the learning readers consulted; unread or absent proof sources remain explicit
authoring obligations. Category placement grants no proof acceptance.

### P-KR-01 — Finite-metric transport duality

**Pages:** finite-metric-transport-duality / finite-metric-transport-duality-examples. **Scope:** For a nonempty finite metric space $X=\{x_1,\ldots,x_m\}$ and probability vectors $p,q$, define couplings $\pi_{ij}\ge0$ with marginals $p,q$, $W_1(p,q)=\min_\pi\sum_{ij}d_{ij}\pi_{ij}$, and prove equality with $\max_{\operatorname{Lip}(f)\le1}\sum_i(p_i-q_i)f_i$. The coupling set is nonempty and compact, so the primal minimum is attained; constants may be quotiented out by $f_1=0$. **Proof route:** Do not cite unspecified LP duality. Let $c_*$ be the primal minimum and form the cone generated by $(e_i,e_j,d_{ij})$ for all $i,j$, together with $(0,0,1)$. Prove this finitely generated cone is closed: reduce every nonnegative representation to one with linearly independent active generators by eliminating a coefficient along a dependence; from a convergent sequence, pass to a subsequence with a fixed active subset, whose coefficients then converge because the generators are independent. For each $\epsilon>0$, $(p,q,c_*-\epsilon)$ is outside the cone. Apply [[thm-strict-separation-of-a-point-from-a-closed-convex-set]] to obtain $(a,b,g)$ with nonpositive pairing on the cone and positive pairing on that point. The slack generator gives $g\le0$; feasibility of a coupling rules out $g=0$, hence $g<0$. Normalize $u_i=a_i/(-g)$, $v_j=b_j/(-g)$, obtaining $u_i+v_j\le d_{ij}$ and $\sum_i p_i u_i+\sum_jq_jv_j>c_*-\epsilon$. Set $f_i=\min_j(d_{ij}-v_j)$; the triangle inequality makes $f$ 1-Lipschitz, while $u_i\le f_i$ and $-f_j\ge v_j$, so its single-potential objective is at least the two-potential value. Normalize $f_1=0$; the finite normalized Lipschitz set is compact, so a maximizer exists. Let $\epsilon\downarrow0$ and combine with weak duality. Complementary slackness follows by summing the nonnegative primal/dual residuals at an attaining pair. **Examples:** two-point space, finite chain metric, and an attaining primal plan/dual potential pair. **Requires:** finite-dimensional Euclidean separation ([[thm-strict-separation-of-a-point-from-a-closed-convex-set]]) and metric/probability definitions; the closed-cone and transport argument above is a local proof design, not a library theorem. **Sources:** This is the first step of the proposed proof for compact Kantorovich–Rubinstein duality. Arjovsky et al., WGAN §§2–3 and proof appendix, PDF pp. 2–8 and 20–25, invoke the compact dual but do not prove it. Santambrogio §§1–2, pp. 1–7, gives definitions and a partial 1-Lipschitz reduction. The source references do not discharge this pair; its full finite proof remains an item-author obligation.

### P-KR-02 — Kantorovich–Rubinstein duality on compact metric spaces

**Pages:** compact-metric-kantorovich-rubinstein-duality / compact-metric-kantorovich-rubinstein-duality-examples. **Scope:** For compact metric $X$ and Borel probability measures $\mu,\nu$, state $W_1(\mu,\nu)=\sup_{\operatorname{Lip}(f)\le1}\{\int f\,d\mu-\int f\,d\nu\}$, with constants quotiented out by fixing $f(x_0)=0$. **Proof route:** For each $\epsilon>0$, choose a finite $\epsilon$-net $F$, order it, and send each point to its nearest net point, breaking ties by the first index; the resulting finite-cell map is Borel and moves every point by at most $\epsilon$. Push any coupling forward to $F\times F$ to obtain $W_1(\mu_F,\nu_F)\le W_1(\mu,\nu)+2\epsilon$. Conversely, lift a finite optimal coupling by the normalized restrictions of $\mu,\nu$ to the corresponding cells (omit zero-mass cells); each lifted pair costs at most its net-pair cost plus $2\epsilon$, so $W_1(\mu,\nu)\le W_1(\mu_F,\nu_F)+2\epsilon$. Apply P-KR-01 on $F$. Extend every finite 1-Lipschitz potential $f_i$ by $F(x)=\min_i(f_i+d(x,x_i))$, which preserves its values on $F$; hence the finite dual value is no larger than the compact dual plus $2\epsilon$ (one integration error for each probability). Conversely, restricting any compact 1-Lipschitz $g$ to $F$ changes each integral by at most $\epsilon$, so the compact dual is at most the finite dual plus $2\epsilon$. Combine these bounds with finite equality and weak duality, then let $\epsilon\downarrow0$. **Examples:** compact interval and two separated point masses. **Requires:** P-KR-01 — Finite-metric transport duality; prove compactness gives a finite net, Borel tie-broken quantization, and the conditional-cell lifting above locally. **Sources:** Santambrogio §§1–2, PDF pp. 1–7, gives definitions and a partial 1-Lipschitz reduction; Arjovsky et al. §§2–3 invoke the compact dual. These are context only: the finite-net proof is a local design and remains an item-author obligation. ML-56 — Integral probability metrics and Wasserstein GANs consumes this pair; ML-61 — Conditional flow matching and transport paths consumes it only if a $W_1$ claim is added. Keep it independent of Category 3.

**Full compact-Borel lifting clarification (owner repair):** Construct products of Borel probabilities on the full product topology locally by measurable sections: distance-defined compact exhaustions of open fibers and the compact-factor tube lemma make open-section evaluation lower semicontinuous; the pi-lambda argument extends this to all product-topology Borel sets, and monotone convergence proves countable additivity. Apply this construction to conditional cell restrictions on the whole compact space. No second-countability or countable-choice premise is added, no Borel-product sigma-algebra identity is presumed, and no compact primal or dual attainment is asserted. Reuse the earlier published pages `metric-spaces`, `compactness-in-metric-spaces`, `compactness`, `subspaces-products-and-quotients`, `sigma-algebras-and-borel-sets`, `measures-and-their-basic-properties`, and `the-lebesgue-integral-and-the-convergence-theorems` for these exact supplier interfaces. Hunter, Measure Theory Chapter 5, is read context for section integration; the full product-topology Borel extension is proved locally.

### P-SDE-01 — Forward Itô SDE well-posedness and density regularity

**Constructive prerequisite module (2026-10-18):** The eight-item Gaussian-smoothing density, score-regularity, Bayes-reversal, Brownian-reconstruction and endpoint-limit design is fully specified in `research/scaffold-refresh-2026-10-18/machine-learning/sde-density-reversal-closure.md`. It is normative for the additive-noise ML-60 branch and supplies explicit hypotheses/arguments without an unnamed parabolic theorem. The richer general conditional contracts below are preserved; they cannot be applied outside their proved density regime.

**2026-10-18 supplier status:** Reuse published `thm-global-strong-well-posedness-for-lipschitz-ito-sdes` and `thm-finite-horizon-moments-for-lipschitz-ito-sdes`. The existing page explicitly supplies no transition-density smoothness, forward-equation uniqueness or time reversal; the constructive addendum below now supplies those density and reversal prerequisites for a precise Gaussian-smoothing branch. General state-dependent density contracts remain conditional and are not inferred from the published existence theorem.

**Pages:** forward-ito-sde-well-posedness / forward-ito-sde-well-posedness-examples. **Scope:** Define an Itô SDE $dX_t=b(t,X_t)dt+\sigma(t,X_t)dW_t$, strong solution, transition density, and forward Kolmogorov equation, with $a=\sigma\sigma^\top$. Separate (i) global strong existence/uniqueness on a complete filtered probability space under Borel time dependence, uniform global Lipschitz-in-state and linear-growth bounds, square-integrable $X_{t_0}$ measurable at $t_0$, and independence of future Brownian increments, from (ii) density regularity: state a separately sourced smoothness/uniform-ellipticity/domain/initial-density theorem that gives a strictly positive smooth $p_t$ and a unique smooth forward Fokker–Planck solution on the stated interval. Anderson also assumes the density of the augmented pair $(X_t,\widetilde W_t)$ and its conditional transition density solve their joint KFP equations uniquely and smoothly; this is a further premise, not a consequence of strong well-posedness. If $X_{t_0}$ may be singular, formulate density and reverse-drift assertions first on $[t_0+\epsilon,T]$; extension to the initial endpoint requires a separately proved limit. **Proof route:** Picard iteration in mean square, coefficient bounds and Grönwall for (i); for (ii), use a named parabolic regularity/heat-kernel theorem with all coefficient, ellipticity, domain, boundary, and initial-data hypotheses matched exactly. Do not infer density smoothness or strict positivity from forward existence. **Examples:** globally Lipschitz scalar drift with nondegenerate constant diffusion; a singular initial law illustrating why smoothness at the initial endpoint is separate. **Requires:** def-d-dimensional-brownian-motion; coordinatewise construction from def-ito-integral-for-square-integrable-predictable-processes and thm-ito-isometry-and-linearity-in-predictable-l2; thm-multidimensional-ito-formula-for-brownian-driven-processes; a strong-solution linear-growth estimate; and a precisely matching PDE density theorem. Vector/matrix Itô integrals must be defined as finite coordinate sums from the existing scalar interface or supplied explicitly. **Sources:** Anderson §§1–4, PDF pp. 1–9, assumes rather than proves forward existence/density regularity and joint-KFP smoothness; Song et al. §§2–4 and appendices pp. 2–5, 13–19, 31–32 use those assumptions. The general density proof is an established-result authoring obligation, not a mathematical open problem. The constructive Gaussian-smoothing addendum supplies a concrete unconditional branch before P-SDE-02/ML-60; broader density regimes require their own precise supplier design.

### P-SDE-03 — Gaussian smoothing densities, scores, and endpoint limits

**Pages:** gaussian-smoothing-densities-scores-and-endpoint-limits / gaussian-smoothing-densities-scores-and-endpoint-limits-examples. **Category:** probability. **Scope and interfaces:** `lem-deterministic-ito-integral-has-gaussian-independent-increments`, `def-gaussian-smoothed-data-law-and-transition-density`, `thm-gaussian-smoothing-density-and-forward-equation`, `thm-gaussian-mixture-score-and-global-lipschitz-bound`, `thm-gaussian-smoothing-endpoint-weak-limit`. These are the five genuinely new probability suppliers numbered 1–4 and 8 in `research/scaffold-refresh-2026-10-18/machine-learning/sde-density-reversal-closure.md`. That complete eight-item module is normative and must be read; its complete hypotheses, formulas, arguments and dependency closure are incorporated here by reference, not reduced to this summary. P-SDE-01 remains the published generic strong-existence/moment supplier and is not reopened or extended. P-SDE-02 separately owns the other three new reverse-kernel/generator/reversal suppliers.

Fix a compactly supported arbitrary Borel initial law mu on R^d, positive finite horizon T, deterministic C1 scalar noise g bounded above and away from zero, and v(t)=integral_0^t g(r)^2dr. The new page proves independent Gaussian integral increments, explicit positive Gaussian-mixture transition/marginal densities and their classical heat equation on [epsilon,T], forward weak-law uniqueness, posterior-mean/covariance score identities and the bound ||D score||<=1/v(epsilon)+R^2/v(epsilon)^2. It also proves E|X_epsilon−X0|^2=d v(epsilon), hence weak convergence and the corresponding W2 coupling estimate without asserting density or bounded score at a singular initial endpoint. The proof routes are respectively deterministic-step approximation/Itô isometry and characteristic functions; positive Gaussian convolution/Tonelli; dominated differentiation and backward heat-test duality; differentiation of explicit posterior density ratios and compact conditional covariance; and the exact Itô second moment. No unspecified parametrix theorem or general state-dependent reversal theorem is imported.

**Requires:** existing P-SDE-01 strong SDE/moment interface; published Brownian/Itô isometry, multivariate Gaussian density and finite characteristic-function uniqueness, Tonelli/dominated convergence and probability-law/weak-convergence interfaces. Exact item-level edges and local alternatives are enumerated in the normative module. No machine-learning result is a supplier. **Examples:** point-mass/finite-mixture initial law; a compactly supported singular law whose positive-time density is smooth; score bounds deteriorating as epsilon decreases; a Gaussian increment coupling giving the endpoint W2 bound. The B page is a leaf and is never a logical prerequisite. **Sources:** explicit Gaussian-convolution/Itô argument in the normative module plus its published local suppliers; inherited Anderson/Song references are context, not a claimed rereading or a substitute for these proofs.

### P-SDE-02 — Anderson reverse-time diffusion

**Required supplier page (2026-10-18):** `gaussian-smoothing-densities-scores-and-endpoint-limits` (P-SDE-03) supplies the five new density/score/endpoint items. The three local reverse-kernel/generator/reversal items numbered 5–7 in the normative module depend on it. No proposed density supplier is hidden on a published page.

**Constructive prerequisite module (2026-10-18):** The eight-item Gaussian-smoothing density, score-regularity, Bayes-reversal, Brownian-reconstruction and endpoint-limit design is fully specified in `research/scaffold-refresh-2026-10-18/machine-learning/sde-density-reversal-closure.md`. It is normative for the additive-noise ML-60 branch and supplies explicit hypotheses/arguments without an unnamed parabolic theorem. The richer general conditional contracts below are preserved; they cannot be applied outside their proved density regime.

**Pages:** anderson-reverse-time-diffusion / anderson-reverse-time-diffusion-examples. **Scope:** Provide two separate reversal contracts. (1) Anderson’s exact finite-interval theorem: let $X_t\in\mathbb R^d$ solve $dX_t=b(X_t,t)dt+\sigma(X_t,t)dW_t$, with a.s.-bounded $X_{t_0}$ independent of the forward Brownian process and coefficients for which the forward SDE has a unique strong solution. Assume its density $p(x,t)$ is a unique smooth solution of the forward KFP equation on $[t_0,T]$, and that the joint density of $(X_t,\widetilde W_t)$ is a unique smooth solution for $t>t_0$ and the corresponding conditional joint density is unique and smooth for $t>s\ge t_0$, where $d\widetilde W_k=dW_k+p(X_t,t)^{-1}\sum_i\partial_{x_i}[p(X_t,t)\sigma_{ik}(X_t,t)]dt$. Require $p>0$ wherever the score is used. Anderson’s $\widetilde W_t-\widetilde W_s$ is independent of $X_t$; its decreasing filtration makes this a reverse-time driver. With $s=T-t$, $a=\sigma\sigma^\top$, and $(\nabla\!\cdot a)_i=\sum_j\partial_{x_j}a_{ij}$, the forward-parameterized reverse SDE has drift $-b(x,T-s)+(\nabla\!\cdot a)(x,T-s)+a(x,T-s)\nabla\log p(x,T-s)$ and diffusion covariance $a(x,T-s)$. (2) Separately provide an unbounded-start reversal theorem for a finite-interval nonexplosive Markov diffusion with square, uniformly nonsingular $\sigma$, $a=\sigma\sigma^\top$, positive smooth transition densities $q(s,x;t,y)$ and positive marginal densities $p_t(y)$ solving the forward equation. Require Chapman–Kolmogorov, the spatial/time regularity and decay/integrability needed to differentiate the transition semigroup and integrate by parts, and finite probability mass. For reversed times $u\le v$, define $K_{u,v}(y,dx)=p_{T-v}(x)q(T-v,x;T-u,y)dx/p_{T-u}(y)$. Prove normalization and reverse Chapman–Kolmogorov from the forward marginal identity and Chapman–Kolmogorov. For $\phi\in C_c^\infty$, derive $\widetilde L_u\phi=p_{T-u}^{-1}\{L_{T-u}^*(p_{T-u}\phi)-\phi L_{T-u}^*p_{T-u}\}$, where $L_t\phi=b_t\cdot\nabla\phi+\tfrac12 a_t:D^2\phi$. Require the resulting reverse martingale problem to be well posed and nonexplosive; localize its generator calculation and reconstruct the Brownian driver from the continuous local martingale using $\sigma^{-1}$ and Lévy’s characterization. Expanding the generator gives reverse drift $-b+\nabla\!\cdot a+a\nabla\log p$ with $[\nabla\!\cdot a]_i=\sum_j\partial_j a_{ij}$. These are explicit hypotheses and a local proof route, not a source-verified theorem; the kernel, generator, martingale-problem, and Brownian-reconstruction proofs remain author obligations. If the data law at $t_0$ may be singular, use the unbounded-start theorem on $[t_0+\epsilon,T]$ only when its positive-density hypotheses hold; convergence to the data endpoint requires a separate weak-limit result. **Proof route:** For (1), reproduce Anderson’s comparison of the forward and reversed joint Fokker–Planck equations, reverse-increment calculation, and Brownian independence proof; transform the backward-Itô equation into the $s=T-t$ forward parameterization and keep the state-dependent divergence correction. For (2), derive the Bayes-reversed kernels, their composition, the generator on compactly supported tests, and then the localized reverse martingale problem and Brownian reconstruction under uniform ellipticity. The scalar time-only diffusion formula is a specialization with $\nabla\!\cdot a=0$. **Examples:** bounded-start Anderson example; stationary Gaussian Ornstein–Uhlenbeck reverse law by direct Gaussian calculation; state-dependent diffusion showing why a score-only correction fails. **Requires:** forward Itô SDE existence, Brownian and componentwise Itô interfaces from P-SDE-01; for the first theorem retain Anderson’s bounded initial state, Brownian independence, forward-density, and unique smooth joint/conditional KFP premises; for the second, retain every density, transition, ellipticity, semigroup, martingale-problem and nonexplosion hypothesis stated above. **Sources:** Anderson, “Reverse-time Diffusion Equation Models,” complete theorem and proof, §§1–4, PDF pp. 1–9 (PDF SHA-256 dcf5cc9261871a586d41e234b390ac0e4c84235abc2d6fa3ea720266a097b87b); Song et al., score-SDE §§2–4 and appendices pp. 2–5, 13–15, 17–19, 31–32, are downstream applications, not forward regularity proofs. The second theorem is a local independent proof design and is not asserted by either consulted source. ML-60 — Score SDEs and reverse-time generation consumes these contracts; no Category 3 computation pair may depend on ML-60.

### P-NUM-ODE-01 — One-step ODE consistency and local error

**2026-10-18 supplier status:** Published supplier reuse: `def-one-step-ode-method-local-defect-order-and-stability` and `thm-forward-euler-one-step-consistency-and-local-error`. Preserve this stable planning coordinate; no second pair or duplicate generic method is proposed.

**Pages:** one-step-ode-consistency-and-local-error / one-step-ode-consistency-and-local-error-examples. **Scope:** Define a one-step method $\Psi_h(t,y)$, local defect $\|\Psi_h(t,y(t))-y(t+h)\|$, and stability. For a fixed interval $[0,T]$ and exact solutions staying in a compact tube, state the order-$p$ hypothesis as a uniform local defect $\le Ch^{p+1}$ and the one-step stability bound $\|\Psi_h(t,u)-\Psi_h(t,v)\|\le(1+Lh)\|u-v\|$. Derive these premises for Euler when $f$ is $C^1$ in time and state with bounded derivatives on that tube; any named Runge–Kutta method must state its order conditions and required derivatives separately. **Proof route:** Taylor-expand the exact flow and the selected method increment, bound remainders uniformly on the tube, and prove the stated stability estimate; do not infer an order from the method label alone. **Examples:** Euler on a scalar linear field; a nonsmooth field where its local order drops. **Requires:** thm-existence-and-uniqueness-of-a-maximal-ode-solution, thm-chain-rule-for-total-derivatives, and a proved Taylor remainder estimate; the local error calculation is explicit, but a numerical-analysis source remains unaudited. **Sources:** Chen et al., neural-ODE Appendix B/C and numerical issues, PDF pp. 13–16, and Papamakarios et al., continuous-flow section pp. 31–33, discuss solver use but do not prove the error theorem. Acquire/read a matching numerical-ODE source before publication. This pair precedes P-NUM-ODE-02.

### P-NUM-SDE-02 — Euler–Maruyama strong and weak convergence

**2026-10-18 supplier status:** Published supplier reuse: `thm-euler-maruyama-strong-rate-under-higham-mao-stuart-hypotheses`, `thm-euler-maruyama-strong-rate-for-square-integrable-initial-data`, and `thm-euler-maruyama-weak-order-one-under-backward-regularity`. These are distinct contracts. The square-integrable theorem is a newly proved global-Lipschitz/time-Hölder alternative, not a weakening of the historical HMS theorem; the weak theorem assumes rather than proves backward regularity. No duplicate authoring of these results is proposed.

**Pages:** euler-maruyama-convergence / euler-maruyama-convergence-examples. **Scope:** For the strong rate, state Higham–Mao–Stuart’s exact sufficient contract: $C^1$ drift and diffusion, one-sided Lipschitz drift, globally Lipschitz diffusion, polynomial local-Lipschitz drift, and uniform finite moments of every order for the exact solution, step process, and continuous Euler–Maruyama interpolation on $[0,T]$. Under these hypotheses, $E\sup_{t\le T}|X(t)-Y(t)|^2=O(h)$, so the grid-point RMS error is $O(h^{1/2})$. A global-Lipschitz/linear-growth specialization may derive these moment premises from an initial condition with all finite moments. Do not state only $X_0\in L^2$ as sufficient for this source theorem. For weak order $1$, state the local theorem through backward Kolmogorov regularity: for the chosen bounded test function $\varphi$, require $u(t,x)=E[\varphi(X_T^{t,x})]$ to solve the backward equation and belong to $C^{2,4}$ with its time derivatives through order two and space derivatives through order four bounded by $C(1+\|x\|^q)$. As a conservative explicit sufficient moment condition for the Taylor remainder and telescoping argument, assume the exact and Euler processes have uniform moments of some order $p\ge 2q+8$ on $[0,T]$, obtained from a finite $p$th moment of $X_0$ under globally Lipschitz/linear-growth coefficients. State a sharper exponent only if separately proved. A claim that bounded $C^4$ coefficients and test function imply this $u$-regularity needs its own stochastic-flow/Kolmogorov regularity proof. For fixed $T$ and $0<h\le1$ with $T/h$ integral, Taylor-expand one Euler step in $t$ and the Gaussian state increment through cubic order; the conditional expectation of the cubic Gaussian term vanishes and the fourth-order remainder is $O(h^2)(1+\|x\|^{q+4})$, while $(\partial_t+L_t)u=0$. Telescope over $T/h$ steps and use the stated moment bound to obtain $|E\varphi(X_T)-E\varphi(X_N)|\le Ch$. This weak proof is a local design, not a conclusion certified by the exposition sources. These are solver bounds for the exact SDE only. They do not cover a learned drift/score bias unless that bias is separately bounded and stable. **Proof route:** use Higham–Mao–Stuart’s stopping/localization, moment truncation, interpolation-defect estimate, Itô formula, Burkholder–Davis–Gundy and Grönwall proof for the strong theorem; for the global-Lipschitz specialization, derive moment bounds separately. For weak order, derive the one-step backward-Kolmogorov cancellation and Gaussian Taylor remainder under the stated $C^{2,4}$ polynomial-growth and explicit $p\ge2q+8$ moment hypotheses; cite or prove a separate result deriving this regularity from coefficient assumptions. For coupled MLMC levels, use shared coarse/fine Brownian increments; a globally Lipschitz payoff then has correction variance $O(h)$ and cost $O(h^{-1})$ per sample, giving the BCS-55 conditional Euler example with $(\alpha,\beta)=(1/2,1)$. If the stronger weak-order bound applies to the payoff, bias exponent $\alpha=1$ is available; do not claim this outside that regime. **Examples:** additive-noise Euler error; a nonzero learned-score bias persisting as $h\to0$. **Requires:** P-NUM-SDE-01 — Euler–Maruyama scheme and coefficient conditions; P-SDE-01 and its exact moment contracts. **Sources:** Higham, Mao, and Stuart, “Strong Convergence of Euler-Type Methods for Nonlinear Stochastic Differential Equations,” §2, Assumption 2.1 and Theorem 2.2 with proof (printed pp. 1042–1046), and §4, Assumptions 3.1/4.1/4.2, Lemma 4.3 and Theorem 4.4 with proof (printed pp. 1054–1056), cached-author copy SHA-256 27d372e331e4c519b0d3b592c230f224c81b4585c21e083f80facd14f39fa0ba. Higham’s “An Algorithmic Introduction to Numerical Simulation of Stochastic Differential Equations,” §4 and §5 (printed pp. 532–538; PDF SHA-256 e02942bc821a5eb63c6b7909aacb87b0e95048f0950e796460878390609566f1), defines strong/weak errors and says Euler–Maruyama has weak order $1$ under “appropriate” conditions, but provides numerical illustrations rather than a matching weak-order theorem/proof. Särkkä and Solin, Applied Stochastic Differential Equations, §8.3 (printed pp. 137–139; PDF SHA-256 279156f8f4382faaa253c27697fcb5ff8d75ace5d940d944553b5f3465ea9435), gives the weak-error definition, Euler scheme, and cites the order-$1$ conclusion under sufficient regularity, but does not supply a full proof; its theorem reference is contextual only. Song et al. pp. 17–19 and 31–32 discuss sampling only. ML-60 — Score SDEs and reverse-time generation consumes this only for sampler-error claims.

### P-WGF-01 — Wasserstein metric and continuity-equation foundations
**Pages:** wasserstein-metric-and-continuity-equation / wasserstein-metric-and-continuity-equation-examples. **Scope:** On $\mathcal P_2(\mathbb R^d)$ define $W_2$, its coupling formula, $W_2$-absolute continuity, metric derivative, and the class $AC^2([0,T];\mathcal P_2)$ of curves with square-integrable metric speed. For such a curve, state the exact dynamic characterization: there is a Borel velocity field $v$ with $\int_0^T\int|v_t|^2\,d\mu_tdt<\infty$ satisfying $\partial_t\mu_t+\nabla\!\cdot(v_t\mu_t)=0$ distributionally against $C_c^\infty((0,T)\times\mathbb R^d)$; every such field gives $|\dot\mu_t|_{W_2}\le\|v_t\|_{L^2(\mu_t)}$ a.e., and the minimal-norm admissible velocity satisfies equality, so the squared metric action is the minimum squared velocity action. Prove the coupling/gluing metric axioms and completeness on $\mathcal P_2$; do not state the smooth-test PDE on arbitrary Polish spaces. **Proof route:** use product couplings for finiteness, gluing plus Minkowski for the triangle inequality, and moment-controlled weak limits for completeness. ### P-WGF-02 — Existence, uniqueness, and stability of Wasserstein gradient flows
**Pages:** wasserstein-gradient-flow-existence-and-stability / wasserstein-gradient-flow-existence-and-stability-examples. **Scope:** State the Chizat–Bach general measure-valued theorem under its full Assumptions 2.1. Let $\mathcal H$ be a separable Hilbert space, $\Omega\subset\mathbb R^d$ the closure of a convex open set, and $F(\mu)=R(\int\Phi\,d\mu)+\int V\,d\mu$ on nonnegative finite measures, with the Bochner integral defined whenever it is finite. Require $R:\mathcal H\to\mathbb R_+$ differentiable, $dR$ Lipschitz on bounded sets and bounded on sublevel sets; $\Phi:\Omega\to\mathcal H$ Frechet differentiable; and $V:\Omega\to\mathbb R_+$ semiconvex. Require nested nonempty closed convex sets $Q_r\subseteq\Omega$ with $\{u\in\Omega:\operatorname{dist}(u,Q_r)\le r'\}\subseteq Q_{r+r'}$, with $\Phi,V$ bounded and $d\Phi$ Lipschitz on every $Q_r$, and constants $C_1,C_2$ such that $\sup_{u\in Q_r}(\|d\Phi_u\|+\|\partial V(u)\|)\le C_1+C_2r$. For $\mu_0\in\mathcal P_2(\Omega)$ concentrated on a $Q_{r_0}$, state Proposition 2.5: a unique global $W_2$-absolutely-continuous curve solves the continuity equation with the negative minimum-norm subgradient velocity of the extended potential $F'(\mu)(u)+\iota_\Omega(u)$, where $F'(\mu)(u)=\langle R'(\int\Phi d\mu),\Phi(u)\rangle+V(u)$. State Theorem 2.6 separately: classical particle subgradient flows initialized in a common $Q_{r_0}$ whose initial empirical measures converge to $\mu_0$ in $W_2$ converge to that unique measure-valued flow in the source’s stated $W_2$ mode. Do not infer global $W_2$-geodesic convexity from linear-mixture convexity. The source’s proof localizes $F$ to measures supported on $Q_r$, proves a second-order Taylor estimate and semiconvexity along generalized geodesics there, identifies strong Wasserstein subdifferentials, applies the imported metric-flow theorem, then uses the linear-growth velocity bound and characteristic-flow/Grönwall estimate to prevent finite-time escape as $r\to\infty$. The particle-limit proof is separate: energy dissipation and bounded-Lipschitz compactness, passage of the velocity fields and continuity equation, and uniqueness identify the limit. Keep global-minimizer claims conditional and separate: Theorem 3.3 requires Assumptions 3.2, positive 2-homogeneity, spherical separation of the initialization, and flow convergence; Theorem 3.5 requires Assumptions 3.4, partial 1-homogeneity, the stated Sard and boundary conditions, box separation, and flow convergence. **Proof route:** reproduce Lemmas B.2–B.4 and Proposition 2.5 with the exact generalized-geodesic Taylor and escape estimates; reproduce Appendix B.3 for the many-particle limit. ### P-RMT-01 — Marchenko–Pastur law and sample-covariance edge bounds

**Pages:** marchenko-pastur-law-and-edge-bounds / marchenko-pastur-law-and-edge-bounds-examples. **Scope:** Let $X\in\mathbb R^{n\times p}$ have iid entries with mean zero, variance one, $n,p\to\infty$ and $p/n\to\gamma\in(0,\infty)$; set $S=X^\top X/n$ and normalize its empirical spectral law by $1/p$. Under iid mean-zero, unit-variance entries with finite fourth moment, state the Marchenko–Pastur weak limit separately from edge convergence: its density is $\sqrt{(b-x)(x-a)}/(2\pi\gamma x)$ on $(a,b)$, where $a=(1-\sqrt\gamma)^2$ and $b=(1+\sqrt\gamma)^2$, and it has atom $(1-1/\gamma)_+$ at zero for the $p\times p$ matrix. This finite-fourth premise is a shared sufficient scope; a weaker second-moment MP theorem is a separate refinement and is not needed here. The MP weak law is not an edge theorem. Under the Bai–Yin finite-fourth-moment edge hypotheses, state almost-sure $\lambda_{\max}(S)\to b$ and $\lambda_{\min}^+(S)\to a$; for $\gamma<1$ there are eventually no structural zeros and $\lambda_{\min}(S)\to a$, whereas for $\gamma>1$ there are $p-n$ exact zero eigenvalues and $\lambda_{\min}^+(S)\to(\sqrt\gamma-1)^2$. At $\gamma=1$ the smallest positive edge tends to zero. Track matrix orientation, normalization, moment assumptions and almost-sure mode separately in each theorem. Weak ESD convergence alone proves none of the extreme-edge limits. **Proof route:** for MP, give a full Stieltjes-transform/resolvent argument: tightness, the leave-one-column resolvent identity, concentration of quadratic forms under the stated moment assumptions, the unique self-consistent transform, and Stieltjes inversion including the zero atom. For the largest edge, use the finite-fourth-moment truncation/high-moment argument of Yin–Bai–Krishnaiah. For the smallest nonzero edge, use the separate Bai–Yin theorem; prove or import its lower-edge argument. Do not use MP weak convergence to infer either edge. **Examples:** the positive spectral support below and above $p/n=1$, structural zeros for $p>n$, and closing lower edge at $\gamma=1$. **Requires:** existing `thm-singular-value-decomposition`, `thm-operator-norm-is-largest-singular-value`, probability weak-convergence and iid moment/strong-law interfaces. The repository has no matching Marchenko–Pastur, Bai–Yin edge, or random-matrix resolvent supplier; fourth-moment quadratic-form and truncation estimates must be supplied with their exact contracts. **Sources:** Yin, Bai, and Krishnaiah, “On the Limit of the Largest Eigenvalue of the Large Dimensional Sample Covariance Matrix,” Theorem 3.1 and complete proof, printed pp. 518–521 (PDF pp. 10–13), source SHA-256 `7580d6bfd8dfbe8884e3072d5f11f19e98c226a51198a484fe44898ca1153dcf`; it proves the largest-edge limit under iid mean-zero entries with finite variance and finite fourth moment, using truncation and high moments, and obtains the lower bound from prior MP-edge results. Bai and Yin, “Limit of the Smallest Eigenvalue of a Large Dimensional Sample Covariance Matrix,” Ann. Probab. 21 (1993), 1275–1294, DOI `10.1214/aop/1176989118`, official record states the smallest/nonzero-edge limit for finite-fourth iid entries. Only its official record/abstract was accessible; Project Euclid’s PDF endpoint was blocked, so its proof was not read. The separate MP proof was not consulted. The separately accepted complete local MP and lower-edge reconstruction below discharges those authoring obligations; the inaccessible original proof remains a historical source-reading limitation.

### P-RMT-02 — Sample-covariance resolvent deterministic equivalents
**Pages:** sample-covariance-resolvent-limits / sample-covariance-resolvent-limits-examples. **Scope:** Define the directional spectral measure by $F_{A_n}^{x_n}(B)=x_n^*\mathbf1_B(A_n)x_n$ for deterministic unit $x_n$. Use Bai–Miao–Pan’s exact Theorem 1 contract: iid complex entries with $EX_{11}=0$, $E|X_{11}|^2=1$, $E|X_{11}|^4<\infty$; $n/N\to c\in(0,\infty)$; deterministic $T_n\succeq0$ with uniformly bounded spectral norm and empirical eigenvalue law $H_n\Rightarrow H$; and deterministic unit $x_n$ satisfying $x_n^*(T_n-zI)^{-1}x_n\to m_H(z)$ off the nonnegative real axis. For $A_n=N^{-1}T_n^{1/2}X_nX_n^*T_n^{1/2}$, the source’s directional measure converges almost surely to the deformed Marchenko–Pastur law $F_{c,H}$. At $T_n=I$, $H=\delta_1$ and the directional premise is automatic, yielding ordinary MP in every deterministic direction. For Hastie’s $S=X^\top X/n$, map $N=n$, dimension $p$, and $X_n=X^\top$; for $r=\|\beta_n\|_2>0$, set $x_n=\beta_n/r$. Fixed negative-axis resolvent integrals follow from bounded continuous test functions. Close the isotropic min-norm risk only when $\gamma\ne1$ and P-RMT-01 supplies a positive nonzero-edge gap $a=(1-\sqrt\gamma)^2$: continuous cutoffs at zero then give the kernel-projection bias and pseudoinverse trace without exchanging a $z\downarrow0$ limit. **Proof route:** Theorem 1’s Section 2 proof truncates/centers finite-fourth-moment entries, controls matrix-norm truncation by a separate spectral-norm theorem, and exposes columns sequentially with $\mathcal F_j=\sigma(s_1,\ldots,s_j)$. Use the adapted full-resolvent martingale decomposition, rank-one identity, fourth-moment quadratic-form bound and Burkholder/Borel–Cantelli concentration, then use the prior MP theorem for the limiting expected transform. Repair the source’s displayed estimates locally: the direct bound after the prefactor $N$ in (2.6) is $O(N^{-1/2})$, still vanishing, and the (2.8) covariance term cannot be dropped; bound $\delta_3$ directly by adapted resolvent concentration/polarization (for isotropic $T_n$, $C_n$ is scalar and bounded). Do not replace the adapted martingale with Rubio–Mestre’s invalid future-dependent leave-one-out claim. Keep risk conditional on design and expectation over independent centered noise distinct. **Examples:** deterministic signal in isotropic iid finite-fourth design; anisotropic $T_n$ only when its directional population-resolvent condition holds; failure of the lower-edge closure at $\gamma=1$. **Requires:** P-RMT-01 — MP weak law and finite-fourth spectral-norm/lower-positive-edge suppliers; fourth-moment quadratic-form inequality and the adapted-resolvent martingale proof. No matching published library result for these matrix concentration contracts was found. **Sources:** Bai, Miao, and Pan, “On Asymptotics of Eigenvectors of Large Sample Covariance Matrix,” Theorem 1 PDF pp. 8–9 and full Section 2 proof pp. 12–16, reprint SHA-256 `2560d318e316fdb8ed677b3f4a5ca6ef7406558e110f9cfd123e4a44e2008c03`; full paper text/read scope and local proof corrections are in `research/statistics-ml-scaffold-audit-2026-10-12/source-recovery/source-findings.json`.
